At the same time he does not care to kW, the opportunities which dull debates afford for dic- tating letters, minutes, e compromise, therefore. lie has set up • phonograph, and Mr. Murray hurries off to his private room and pours hi. confidences into the receiver of his phonograph. If a division demands his return to the legislative clamber bonen Arrest the progress of the cylinder in a moment and resume work at hi. convenience. The first thing in She morning his sivretary fetches the cylinder used the previous eveniag,and, placing it in • duplicate phonograph which is kept at Dover blouse, he proceeds to writ, from its dictation. ! I'Mere is, of course, an element of delay in this, but, seeing that all urgent business is transacted while the secretary is in e•idence in the daytime. the oblection ens hardly be regarded as serious. When the Lord Advocate is in Scotland the phonograph plays an important part in his daily work, and he dclares, quite frankly, that he could scarcely get lon without it now.

I We have thin week to record with regret the sudden demise of Mr. Thom. Williams, farmer of New Hey farm. Neetoo-le-Willowe at the 4ge of • 7r, years, which took place at his residence on Wed- ' nestle), night shortly after II o'clock.
